### Anthropic's IPO filing shows soaring sales and steep losses
- **Source**: France 24 - International breaking news, top stories and headlines

- **Date**: 2026-10-26 22:01
- **Link**: https://www.france24.com/en/tv-shows/business/20261026-anthropic-s-ipo-filing-shows-soaring-sales-and-steep-losses
- **Summary**: AI giant Anthropic has publicly filed for an initial public offering (IPO) with US market regulators. The document offered a glimpse into the company's finances but also contained nearly 100 pages related to risks around investing in the company. The filing revealed that in the first half of 2026, its revenue soared to $16.3 billion while net loss widened to over $80 billion. Plus, London's iconic Oxford Street, one of Europe's busiest shopping areas, has been partially pedestrianised.

- **Source**: Middle East and north Africa | The Guardian
- **Author**: Sin├⌐ad Campbell
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 21:39
- **Link**: https://www.theguardian.com/world/2026/oct/26/british-politicians-among-international-delegates-barred-from-entering-west-bank
- **Summary**: Group was on visit to meet Palestinian children, farmers and civil leaders facing escalating violence from Israeli forcesAn international delegation including British politicians has been barred from entering the West Bank on a trip to meet Palestinian children, farmers and society leaders facing escalating violence from Israeli forces during olive harvest season.The delegation of 23 people, including four elected representatives, was drawn from countries including France, Spain, Luxembourg, Switzerland, Greece, Holland, Ireland and the US. It included the Labour MP Clive Lewis, the Scottish National party MP Lara Bird, the Scottish Greens MSP Kate Nevens and the former Labour MP Sam Tarry. Continue reading...

### ΓÇÿWe left with heavy heartsΓÇÖ: IsraelΓÇÖs assault displaces thousands in Hebron
- **Source**: Section Feed
- **Author**: Hossam Desouky
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 21:34
- **Link**: https://www.middleeasteye.net/live-blog/live-blog-update/we-left-heavy-hearts-israels-assault-displaces-thousands-hebron
- **Summary**: ΓÇÿWe left with heavy heartsΓÇÖ: IsraelΓÇÖs assault displaces thousands in Hebron Less than two weeks before her wedding, Mayar Amrish sat with her family in the spacious courtyard of their home, making plans for the big day. The 23-year-old Palestinian was preparing for her future, but within hours, her family home would be reduced to rubble. Outside the house in Farsh al-Hawa, in the occupied West Bank city of Hebron, Israeli forces were launching a sweeping demolition and forced displacement campaign. The assault, condemned as collective punishment against Palestinians, followed a shooting on Friday in which two Palestinians killed two Israeli soldiers. Israel imposed a total lockdown on the city and launched a wide-ranging military assault involving home raids, arrests, demolitions and the forced displacement of thousands of people. ### 'Shithouse countries': Trump has pledged not to use such language again, adviser says
- **Source**: Section Feed

- **Summary**: 'Shithouse countries': Trump has pledged not to use such language again, adviser says Submitted by Yasmine El-Sabawi on Mon, 10/26/2026 - 15:53 The US president, campaigning for Republicans across the country, said he doesn't want immigrants from Libya, Yemen, and Somalia coming to US US President Donald Trump delivers remarks during a midterm elections rally at McMorran Place Sports and Entertainment Center in Port Huron, Michigan, on 25 October 2026 (Alex Wong/Getty Images/AFP) Off After US President Donald Trump called a number of Arab nations "shithouse countries" on Saturday, his newly minted adviser on Arab-American affairs said the American president has since promised he wouldn't do it again. "Libya, Yemen, Haiti, Somalia. Remember what I called them? Shithouse countries," Trump told a crowd of supporters at a rally in Perry, Georgia, where he is drumming up support for Republican candidates ahead of the 3 November midterm elections. He added that the countries are "terror-infested". "Why do we always have to take people from shithouse countries? Why can't we take people from like, well, Norway, Sweden? Why not Denmark?" Amer Ghalib, his Yemeni-born adviser, wrote on his Facebook page on Sunday that he "contacted the president via text message" to express his "dismay and rejection of those remarks". He said he later met with the president ahead of his rally in Port Huron, Michigan, on Sunday, and "reiterated my request". "He expressed understanding of the situation...

### Serbian student movement eyes power after election triumph
- **Source**: France 24 - International breaking news, top stories and headlines
- **Author**: FRANCE24
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 19:48
- **Link**: https://www.france24.com/en/serbian-student-movement-eyes-power-after-election-triumph
- **Summary**: Emotions ran high in Belgrade on Monday after a student-led movement upset the right-wing populist Aleksandar Vucic's party in Serbia's snap election. A celebratory mood gripped the capital, with drivers honking their horns and passers-by holding up three fingers -- the winning list's ballot number and a Serbian greeting. Many wore badges and other symbols of the student movement, while a large group of residents spent the day cleaning up a park between parliament and the presidency after Vucic loyalists, who had camped there for more than a year, fled. FRANCE 24's Mark Owen speaks with researchers and political activist Aleksandar Matkovi─ç.

- **Summary**: GENEVA, Oct 26 (Reuters) - Israel continued to commit genocide in Gaza after last October's US-brokered ceasefire in the enclave, a UN commission said in a report published on Monday that Israel dismissed as part of a "misinformation campaign".The report by the UN Independent International Commission of Inquiry on the Occupied Palestinian Territory, including East Jerusalem, and Israel examined violations against Palestinian children since the start of the war between Israel and Hamas on October 7, 2023 up to September this year.

### Iran's supreme leader: A strong Iran rests upon scientific power
- **Source**: Section Feed
- **Author**: Hossam Desouky
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 18:46
- **Link**: https://www.middleeasteye.net/live-blog/live-blog-update/irans-supreme-leader-strong-iran-rests-upon-scientific-power
- **Summary**: Iran's supreme leader: A strong Iran rests upon scientific power In a statement shared by Iran's semi-official Tasnim news agency on Monday, Iranian Supreme Leader Mojtaba Khamenei emphasised the importance of scientific progress to Iran's strength. "The key to making Iran even stronger is the boundary-breaking work and problem-solving of the country's elites in scientific matters," Khamenei said in the statement addressed to Iranian elites and scientific and sporting medalists competing internationally. "The malice and crimes committed by the enemies of Islamic Iran against its scientists and elites demonstrate the effective role of elites in strengthening the country," Khamenei added, calling on officials to "provide necessary infrastructure" for a deep and rapid scientific leap. The statement was read at a ceremony honouring Iranian scientific and sports medalists, Tasnim reported.

### 'A Zionist framing of Palestinian history': Why a book on Palestine was pulled from its publisher
- **Source**: Section Feed
- **Author**: Mounira Elsamra
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 18:34
- **Link**: https://www.middleeasteye.net/news/zionist-framing-palestinian-history-authors-pull-palestine-book-random-house
- **Summary**: 'A Zionist framing of Palestinian history': Why a book on Palestine was pulled from its publisher Submitted by Mounira Elsamra on Fri, 10/23/2026 - 20:38 Authors Alana Hadid and Jenan Matari say the publisher made 'inaccurate' unilateral changes to their manuscript, including descriptions of 7 October 2023 Jenan Matari, a Palestinian-American activist, sits for a photograph at Haraz Coffee in New Brunswick, New Jersey, on 15 September 2025 (USA Today Network via Reuters Connect) Off A book deal with Penguin Random House, the world's largest book publisher, was a dream for two Palestinian authors who had spent the last two years working on a book detailing Palestinian history. Alana Hadid and Jenan Matari are both vocal Palestinian-American activists with sizable online audiences. For them, the deal with Penguin Random House was an opportunity to educate many young readers about Palestinian oppression. Matari, who also spoke on behalf of Hadid, told Middle East Eye they believed their work could impact young readers' perspectives on the Palestinian struggle for liberation from the illegal Israeli occupying force and give them tools to call out oppression. The book was set to be titled Displacement and the Palestinian American Story, part of an ongoing series of non-fiction books for young teens. "Kids will eventually learn to connect dots very easily... and they will learn how to voice their rejection of other people's oppression," Matari said. But that deal came to a halt ...

### Iran, Saudi Arabia, Pakistan, Turkey to cooperate on regional security
- **Source**: Section Feed
- **Author**: Hossam Desouky
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 17:46
- **Link**: https://www.middleeasteye.net/live-blog/live-blog-update/iran-saudi-arabia-pakistan-turkey-cooperate-regional-security
- **Summary**: Iran, Saudi Arabia, Pakistan, Turkey to cooperate on regional security Iran, Saudi Arabia, Pakistan, and Turkey agreed to continue dialogue to strengthen cooperation after their quadrilateral meeting on Monday in Tehran, according to a joint statement. The meeting, which focused on "peace and security in the region," was hosted by Iran's Supreme National Security Council Secretary Mohsen Rezaei and attended by President Masoud Pezeshkian and Foreign Minister Abbas Araghchi, as well as Saudi Defence Minister Khalid bin Salman, Pakistani army chief Field Marshal Asim Munir, and Turkey's intelligence chief Ibrahim Kalin.

- **Source**: Section Feed
- **Author**: MEE staff
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 17:42

- **Summary**: FBI considering espionage case against Saudi OpenAI suspect: Report Submitted by MEE staff on Mon, 10/26/2026 - 14:51 Engineer Abdulrahman Alfozan is accused of secretly recording conversations in the company's San Francisco conference room Abdulrahman Alfozan, a Saudi national who worked as an OpenAI engineer, allegedly looks behind a TV where a recording device had been placed in OpenAI offices in San Francisco, California, in a still image obtained from surveillance video on 5 October 2026 (Department of Justice/Handout via Reuters) Off The FBI is considering adding an economic espionage charge against Abdulrahman Alfozan, the Saudi national accused of secretly recording conversations in an OpenAI executive conference room, according to a law-enforcement official who spoke to The Wall Street Journal (WSJ). The probe questions whether Alfozan, who worked at OpenAI as an engineer, may have stolen information from the company to offer to the Saudi government, even if he wasn't recruited to do so, the official told the WSJ in an article published on Saturday. Alfozan's lawyer, William Pernik, told the WSJ that he hadn't received any information from the government about additional charges. Alfozan, 35, a Saudi national and a legal permanent resident of the US, is facing federal charges. He was arrested earlier this month and accused of wrapping a thumb-sized device containing a microphone and camera in toilet paper and plugging it behind a television in the meeting room, mo...

### UN human rights chief warns of 'ethnic cleansing' in Hebron
- **Source**: Section Feed
- **Author**: Hossam Desouky
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 17:04
- **Link**: https://www.middleeasteye.net/live-blog/live-blog-update/un-human-rights-chief-warns-ethnic-cleansing-hebron
- **Summary**: UN human rights chief warns of 'ethnic cleansing' in Hebron UN human rights chief Volker Turk said Israeli threats to empty neighbourhoods in Hebron raise "serious concerns of ethnic cleansing". Turk's comments come as an Israeli operation in the occupied West Bank city of Hebron, which began on Friday, has forcibly displaced more than 2,000 Palestinians from the city. "The sweeping steps taken raise grave concerns of unnecessary and disproportionate use of force, which must end," said Turk. "Collective punishment is a grave violation of international law," he added.

- **Summary**: Egypt's parliament authorised on Monday sending military support to Saudi Arabia to defend the kingdom from attacks by Yemen's Houthis, following a request from President Abdel Fattah al-Sisi. The House of Representatives voted with a two-thirds majority in favour of "authorising President Abdel Fattah al-Sisi to take the necessary measures to support the Kingdom of Saudi Arabia", according to a parliament statement. The parliament, stacked with supporters of Sisi, had held three rare secret meetings on the matter and postponed the vote twice.

- **Source**: AL-MONITOR: The Pulse of The Middle East
- **Author**:

- **Link**: https://www.al-monitor.com/originals/2026/10/no-fuel-keep-babies-warm-shortages-push-hospitals-yemens-taiz-brink
- **Summary**: By Olivia Le PoidevinGENEVA, Oct 26 (Reuters) - Fuel shortages in Yemen's besieged city of Taiz have forced one hospital to cut the number of incubators it can operate from 40 to 10, putting newborn babies at risk as weeks of fighting choke off vital supplies and deepen a humanitarian crisis affecting hundreds of thousands of people."There's no oxygen, there's no fuel to run the incubators, to keep the babies warm," said Nada Ahmed, a nurse in the neonatal unit of the Yemeni-Swedish Hospital for Maternity and Childhood.

- **Source**: Section Feed
- **Author**: Mee User
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 14:41
- **Link**: https://www.middleeasteye.net/live-blog/live-blog-update/blasts-hit-yemens-capital-and-red-sea-coast-residents-report
- **Summary**: Blasts hit YemenΓÇÖs capital and Red Sea coast, residents report Explosions have been reported in YemenΓÇÖs Houthi-held capital Sanaa and along the countryΓÇÖs Red Sea coast, with residents describing damage to homes and a hospital, Al Jazeera reported. Residents in western Sanaa reported hearing at least four blasts, although it was not immediately clear what had been hit, the report said. A woman living near a Houthi base said a powerful explosion shattered windows in her home and neighbouring houses. In the coastal city of Mokha, resident Khaled Adib said a rocket struck a four-storey residential building, damaging at least 10 other houses and Mocha General Hospital, according to residents. Meanwhile, fighting continues between Houthi forces and Saudi-backed Yemeni government troops around the strategic city of Taiz.

- **Summary**: Lana Nusseibeh has been the face of a disastrous Emirates policy that has prolonged war and death. It is a scandal that she is even being consideredOver the past few years, you can plot the plunge into global instability not only through the unchecked assaults on international law and civilian life, but through the decline and undermining of the authority and credibility of international institutions. The latest level in this spiral is the proposed appointment of Lana Nusseibeh of the United Arab Emirates (UAE) as the UN secretary general. Her bid has been rapidly gaining momentum and endorsements. And it has been met with outrage among the Sudanese.Nusseibeh is the minister of state at the UAE and a former UAE ambassador to the UN. For the past three years, the country has been connected to ΓÇô bear with me, this will be a long sentence ΓÇô supporting the Rapid Support Forces (RSF) militia in Sudan, facilitating mercenaries to fight alongside the militia (which is accused of ethnic cleansing, crimes against humanity and sexual violence), and appropriating international aid infrastructure to smuggle weapons into the country. ### I saw a drone suddenly descend towards me. My heart started pounding and I thought, ΓÇÿIs this the end of my life?ΓÇÖ
- **Source**: Middle East and north Africa | The Guardian
- **Author**: Elsy Moufarrej in Tyre
- **Date**: 2026-10-26 05:00

- **Summary**: Photographer Zeinab Faraj, 21, found herself buried under rubble beside a dying colleague while reporting in Lebanon. She had never expected to be the target of an Israeli missile attack.It was mid-April and after a month of deadly airstrikes across Lebanon, Israel agreed to a ceasefire. Much of the bombing had focused on southern Lebanon, where entire villages had been demolished. But with the truce, people were hopeful of being able to return to their homes or to see what remained.Zeinab Faraj, a 21-year-old photojournalist, decided to join Amal Khalil, 42, a newspaper reporter in Lebanon, on a trip to an area in the south of the country to document the aftermath of war. The two had worked together on a number of assignments after Khalil had seen FarajΓÇÖs work on social media and contacted her. Continue reading...

Langer grabbed a tissue that had been exposed to the substance ΓÇô a piece of evidence, perhaps, in a future legal case. Then, on impulse, she lifted it to her face and inhaled deeply. Her hope, she wrote later, was that doing so might enable a doctor to identify the toxin.This anecdote appears in Anti-Zionism: A Jewish History, by Benjamin Moser. In it, the Pulitzer prize-winning author profiles a dozen Jews who actively opposed Zionism, before and after the establishment of Israel ΓÇô from Aaron Samuel Tamares, a Polish rabbi who began inveighing against Jewish nationalism in the late 19th century; to British diplomat Edwin Montagu, who sought to derail the 1917 Balfour declaration promising a Jewish state in Palestine; to Israeli ΓÇ£new historianΓÇ¥ Avi Shlaim ΓÇô figures he sees as part of ΓÇ£a hidden tradition ΓÇª of people who, though long dismissed and despised, were ultimately proven rightΓÇ¥.
